![Image 702: \displaystyle  = \sum_{d|a_1,a_2} \mu(d);](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%3D+%5Csum_%7Bd%7Ca_1%2Ca_2%7D+%5Cmu%28d%29%3B&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

inserting this and writing ![Image 703: {a_1 = d c_1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Ba_1+%3D+d+c_1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 704: {a_2 = d c_2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Ba_2+%3D+d+c_2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we can now write [(30)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dor) as

![Image 705: \displaystyle  \sum_{b|P} g(b) \sum_{d|P/b} \mu(d) g(d)^2 \sum_{c_1,c_2 | P/db} \rho_{dbc_1} g(c_1) \rho_{dbc_2} g(c_1)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bb%7CP%7D+g%28b%29+%5Csum_%7Bd%7CP%2Fb%7D+%5Cmu%28d%29+g%28d%29%5E2+%5Csum_%7Bc_1%2Cc_2+%7C+P%2Fdb%7D+%5Crho_%7Bdbc_1%7D+g%28c_1%29+%5Crho_%7Bdbc_2%7D+g%28c_1%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

which now does factor as a sum of squares:

![Image 706: \displaystyle  \sum_{b|P} g(b) \sum_{d|P/b} \mu(d) g(d)^2 \left( \sum_{c | P/db} \rho_{dbc} g(c)\right)^2.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bb%7CP%7D+g%28b%29+%5Csum_%7Bd%7CP%2Fb%7D+%5Cmu%28d%29+g%28d%29%5E2+%5Cleft%28+%5Csum_%7Bc+%7C+P%2Fdb%7D+%5Crho_%7Bdbc%7D+g%28c%29%5Cright%29%5E2.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Writing ![Image 707: {e = bcd}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Be+%3D+bcd%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), this becomes

![Image 708: \displaystyle  \sum_{bd|P} \frac{\mu(d)}{g(b)} \left( \sum_{bd | e | P} \rho_e g(e)\right)^2.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bbd%7CP%7D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Cmu%28d%29%7D%7Bg%28b%29%7D+%5Cleft%28+%5Csum_%7Bbd+%7C+e+%7C+P%7D+%5Crho_e+g%28e%29%5Cright%29%5E2.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Writing ![Image 709: {bd = m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bbd+%3D+m%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), this becomes [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 710: \displaystyle \sum_{m|P} h(m) y_m^2 \ \ \ \ \ (31)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bm%7CP%7D+h%28m%29+y_m%5E2+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2831%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) where

![Image 711: \displaystyle  y_m := \frac{\mu(m)}{h(m)} \sum_{m | e | P} \rho_e g(e)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++y_m+%3A%3D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Cmu%28m%29%7D%7Bh%28m%29%7D+%5Csum_%7Bm+%7C+e+%7C+P%7D+%5Crho_e+g%28e%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and ![Image 712: {h}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bh%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is defined on factors of ![Image 713: {P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) by the Dirichlet convolution

![Image 714: \displaystyle  \frac{1}{h} = \mu * \frac{1}{g}.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bh%7D+%3D+%5Cmu+%2A+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bg%7D.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Thus ![Image 715: {h}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bh%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the multiplicative function with [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 716: \displaystyle h(p) := \frac{g(p)}{1-g(p)} \ \ \ \ \ (32)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++h%28p%29+%3A%3D+%5Cfrac%7Bg%28p%29%7D%7B1-g%28p%29%7D+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2832%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) for all ![Image 717: {p|P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7CP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002); we extend ![Image 718: {h}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bh%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) by zero outside of the factors of ![Image 719: {P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). In particular ![Image 720: {h}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bh%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is non-negative.

The coefficients ![Image 721: {(y_m)_{m|P}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28y_m%29_%7Bm%7CP%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) are a transform of the original coefficients ![Image 722: {(\rho_e)_{e|P}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28%5Crho_e%29_%7Be%7CP%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). For instance, if ![Image 723: {P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) was replaced by a single prime ![Image 724: {p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), then the coefficients ![Image 725: {(y_1,y_p)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28y_1%2Cy_p%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) would be related to the coefficients ![Image 726: {(\rho_1,\rho_p)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28%5Crho_1%2C%5Crho_p%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) by the transform

![Image 727: \displaystyle  y_1 = \rho_1 + g(p) \rho_p; \quad y_p = -\frac{g(p)}{h(p)} \rho_p](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++y_1+%3D+%5Crho_1+%2B+g%28p%29+%5Crho_p%3B+%5Cquad+y_p+%3D+-%5Cfrac%7Bg%28p%29%7D%7Bh%28p%29%7D+%5Crho_p&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

which is inverted as

![Image 728: \displaystyle  \rho_1 = y_1 + h(p) y_p; \quad \rho_p = - \frac{1}{g(p)} h(p) y_p.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Crho_1+%3D+y_1+%2B+h%28p%29+y_p%3B+%5Cquad+%5Crho_p+%3D+-+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bg%28p%29%7D+h%28p%29+y_p.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

More generally, we have the following inversion formula:

> **Exercise 28 (Möbius-type inversion formula)** Verify that [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> 
> [![Image 729: \displaystyle \rho_e = \frac{\mu(e)}{g(e)} \sum_{e|m|P} h(m) y_m \ \ \ \ \ (33)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Crho_e+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Cmu%28e%29%7D%7Bg%28e%29%7D+%5Csum_%7Be%7Cm%7CP%7D+h%28m%29+y_m+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2833%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) for any ![Image 730: {e|P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Be%7CP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002).

From this formula, we see in particular that ![Image 731: {y_m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By_m%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is supported on ![Image 732: {m \leq R}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm+%5Cleq+R%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) if and only if ![Image 733: {\rho_e}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Crho_e%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is supported on ![Image 734: {e \leq R}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Be+%5Cleq+R%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). The constraint ![Image 735: {\rho_1=1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Crho_1%3D1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) can now be written in terms of the ![Image 736: {y_m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By_m%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) coefficients as [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 737: \displaystyle \sum_{m|P} h(m) y_m = 1. \ \ \ \ \ (34)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bm%7CP%7D+h%28m%29+y_m+%3D+1.+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2834%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) Our task is now to minimise the quadratic form [(31)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#mp) amongst all coefficients ![Image 738: {y_m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By_m%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) supported on ![Image 739: {m \leq R}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm+%5Cleq+R%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) obeying [(34)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#linear). The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives the lower bound

![Image 740: \displaystyle  \sum_{m|P} h(m) y_m^2 \geq J^{-1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bm%7CP%7D+h%28m%29+y_m%5E2+%5Cgeq+J%5E%7B-1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

where ![Image 741: {J := \sum_{m|P: m \leq R} h(m)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BJ+%3A%3D+%5Csum_%7Bm%7CP%3A+m+%5Cleq+R%7D+h%28m%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), with equality precisely when ![Image 742: {y_m = 1/J}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By_m+%3D+1%2FJ%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for ![Image 743: {m \leq R}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm+%5Cleq+R%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Inserting this back into [(33)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#rhoe), we arrive at the optimised Selberg sieve coefficients [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 744: \displaystyle \rho_e = \frac{1}{J} \frac{\mu(e)}{g(e)} \sum_{e|m|P: m \leq R} h(m) \ \ \ \ \ (35)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Crho_e+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7BJ%7D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Cmu%28e%29%7D%7Bg%28e%29%7D+%5Csum_%7Be%7Cm%7CP%3A+m+%5Cleq+R%7D+h%28m%29+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2835%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) and the quantity [(14)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dplus) is equal to ![Image 745: {\frac{1}{J}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7BJ%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002).

We have a pleasant size bound on the coefficients ![Image 746: {\rho_e}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Crho_e%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002):

> **Lemma 29** We have ![Image 747: {|\rho_e| \leq 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7C%5Crho_e%7C+%5Cleq+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for all ![Image 748: {e|P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Be%7CP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002).

_Proof:_ Writing ![Image 749: {m = ek}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm+%3D+ek%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we have

![Image 750: \displaystyle  |\rho_e| = \frac{1}{Jg(e)} \sum_{k| P/e: k \leq R/e} h(e) h(k) ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7C%5Crho_e%7C+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7BJg%28e%29%7D+%5Csum_%7Bk%7C+P%2Fe%3A+k+%5Cleq+R%2Fe%7D+h%28e%29+h%28k%29+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

but

![Image 751: \displaystyle  J \geq \sum_{k \leq R/e} \sum_{d|e} h(d) h(k) ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++J+%5Cgeq+%5Csum_%7Bk+%5Cleq+R%2Fe%7D+%5Csum_%7Bd%7Ce%7D+h%28d%29+h%28k%29+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

![Image 752: \displaystyle  = \sum_{k \leq R/e} h(k) \frac{h(e)}{g(e)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%3D+%5Csum_%7Bk+%5Cleq+R%2Fe%7D+h%28k%29+%5Cfrac%7Bh%28e%29%7D%7Bg%28e%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

since one can compute that ![Image 753: {\frac{h}{g} = h * 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cfrac%7Bh%7D%7Bg%7D+%3D+h+%2A+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). The claim follows. ![Image 754: \Box](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5CBox&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

The upper bound sieve coefficients ![Image 755: {\lambda^+_d}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Clambda%5E%2B_d%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) in [(28)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#selberg) is then bounded by

![Image 756: \displaystyle  |\lambda^+_d| \leq \sum_{[d_1,d_2]=d} 1 = \tau_3(d)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7C%5Clambda%5E%2B_d%7C+%5Cleq+%5Csum_%7B%5Bd_1%2Cd_2%5D%3Dd%7D+1+%3D+%5Ctau_3%28d%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

where ![Image 757: {\tau_3(d) = \sum_{d_1 d_2 d_3 = d} 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Ctau_3%28d%29+%3D+%5Csum_%7Bd_1+d_2+d_3+%3D+d%7D+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the third divisor function. Applying Theorem [5](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dual-sieve), we thus have a general upper bound for Problem [3](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#sivp):

> **Theorem 30 (Selberg sieve upper bound)**[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) Let ![Image 758: {P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be a squarefree natural number. For each prime ![Image 759: {p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) dividing ![Image 760: {P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), let ![Image 761: {E_p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BE_p%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be a set of integers. Let ![Image 762: {g}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bg%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be a multiplicative function with ![Image 763: {0 < g(p) < 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B0+%3C+g%28p%29+%3C+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for all ![Image 764: {p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and define the multiplicative function ![Image 765: {h}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bh%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) by [(32)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#hp), extending ![Image 766: {h}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bh%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) by zero outside of the factors of ![Image 767: {P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Let ![Image 768: {D = R^2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BD+%3D+R%5E2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for some ![Image 769: {R \geq 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BR+%5Cgeq+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Suppose that ![Image 770: {(a_n)_{n \in {\bf Z}}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28a_n%29_%7Bn+%5Cin+%7B%5Cbf+Z%7D%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is a finitely supported sequence of non-negative reals, such that [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> 
> [![Image 771: \displaystyle \sum_{n \in E_d} a_n = g(d) X + r_d \ \ \ \ \ (36)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bn+%5Cin+E_d%7D+a_n+%3D+g%28d%29+X+%2B+r_d+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2836%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) for all ![Image 772: {d \leq D}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bd+%5Cleq+D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) and some reals ![Image 773: {X, r_d}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BX%2C+r_d%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Then
> 
> 
> ![Image 774: \displaystyle  \sum_{n \not \in\bigcup_{p|P} E_p} a_n \leq \frac{X}{\sum_{d \leq R} h(d)} + \sum_{d \leq D} \tau_3(d) |r_d|. ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bn+%5Cnot+%5Cin%5Cbigcup_%7Bp%7CP%7D+E_p%7D+a_n+%5Cleq+%5Cfrac%7BX%7D%7B%5Csum_%7Bd+%5Cleq+R%7D+h%28d%29%7D+%2B+%5Csum_%7Bd+%5Cleq+D%7D+%5Ctau_3%28d%29+%7Cr_d%7C.+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

> **Remark 31** To compare this bound against the “expected” value of ![Image 775: {X \prod_{p|P} (1-g(p))}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BX+%5Cprod_%7Bp%7CP%7D+%281-g%28p%29%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), observe from Euler products that
> 
> 
> ![Image 776: \displaystyle \prod_{p|P} (1-g(p)) = \frac{1}{\sum_d h(d)}.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle+%5Cprod_%7Bp%7CP%7D+%281-g%28p%29%29+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B%5Csum_d+h%28d%29%7D.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

The quantity ![Image 777: {\sum_{d \leq R} h(d)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Csum_%7Bd+%5Cleq+R%7D+h%28d%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) can often be computed in practice using standard methods for controlling sums of multiplicative functions, such as Theorem 27 of [Notes 1](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2014/11/23/254a-notes-1-elementary-multiplicative-number-theory/). We illustrate this with the following sample application of the Selberg sieve:

> **Theorem 32 (Sieving an interval)**[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) Let ![Image 778: {k, C}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%2C+C%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be fixed natural numbers, and let ![Image 779: {x \geq 2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx+%5Cgeq+2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). For each prime number ![Image 780: {p \leq \sqrt{x}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7Bx%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), let ![Image 781: {E_p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BE_p%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be the union of ![Image 782: {\omega(p)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) residue classes modulo ![Image 783: {p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), where ![Image 784: {\omega(p) = k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Comega%28p%29+%3D+k%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for all ![Image 785: {p \geq C}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp+%5Cgeq+C%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and ![Image 786: {\omega(p) < p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Comega%28p%29+%3C+p%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for all ![Image 787: {p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Then for any ![Image 788: {\varepsilon > 0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon+%3E+0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), one has [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> 
> [![Image 789: \displaystyle |\{ n: n \leq x \} \backslash \bigcup_{p \leq \sqrt{x}} E_p| \leq (2^k k! + \varepsilon) {\mathfrak S} \frac{x}{\log^k x} \ \ \ \ \ (37)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7C%5C%7B+n%3A+n+%5Cleq+x+%5C%7D+%5Cbackslash+%5Cbigcup_%7Bp+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7Bx%7D%7D+E_p%7C+%5Cleq+%282%5Ek+k%21+%2B+%5Cvarepsilon%29+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Cfrac%7Bx%7D%7B%5Clog%5Ek+x%7D+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2837%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) whenever ![Image 790: {x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is sufficiently large depending on ![Image 791: {k,C,\varepsilon}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%2CC%2C%5Cvarepsilon%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), where ![Image 792: {{\mathfrak S}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the singular series [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> 
> [![Image 793: \displaystyle {\mathfrak S} := \prod_p (1-\frac{1}{p})^{-k} (1 - \frac{\omega(p)}{p}). \ \ \ \ \ (38)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%3A%3D+%5Cprod_p+%281-%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%7D%29%5E%7B-k%7D+%281+-+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%7D%29.+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2838%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

_Proof:_ We apply Theorem [30](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#ssu) with ![Image 794: {a_n := 1_{1 \leq n \leq x}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Ba_n+%3A%3D+1_%7B1+%5Cleq+n+%5Cleq+x%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 795: {X := x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BX+%3A%3D+x%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 796: {P := \prod_{p \leq \sqrt{x}} p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP+%3A%3D+%5Cprod_%7Bp+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7Bx%7D%7D+p%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and ![Image 797: {g(p) := \frac{\omega(p)}{p}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bg%28p%29+%3A%3D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). We set ![Image 798: {D := x^{1-\varepsilon}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BD+%3A%3D+x%5E%7B1-%5Cvarepsilon%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) and ![Image 799: {R := D^{1/2}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BR+%3A%3D+D%5E%7B1%2F2%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). For any ![Image 800: {d|P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bd%7CP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 801: {E_d}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BE_d%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the union of ![Image 802: {\prod_{p|d} \omega(p)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cprod_%7Bp%7Cd%7D+%5Comega%28p%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) residue classes modulo ![Image 803: {d}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bd%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and thus by [(36)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dad) we have

![Image 804: \displaystyle  |r_d| \leq \prod_{p|d} \omega(p) ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7Cr_d%7C+%5Cleq+%5Cprod_%7Bp%7Cd%7D+%5Comega%28p%29+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and in particular

![Image 805: \displaystyle  |r_d| \ll_{k,C} \tau_k(d).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7Cr_d%7C+%5Cll_%7Bk%2CC%7D+%5Ctau_k%28d%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

From Theorem [30](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#ssu), we thus can upper bound the left-hand side of [(37)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#stab) by

![Image 806: \displaystyle  \frac{x}{\sum_{d \leq R} h(d)} + O_{k,C}( \sum_{d \leq D} \tau_3(d) \tau_k(d) ).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cfrac%7Bx%7D%7B%5Csum_%7Bd+%5Cleq+R%7D+h%28d%29%7D+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%7D%28+%5Csum_%7Bd+%5Cleq+D%7D+%5Ctau_3%28d%29+%5Ctau_k%28d%29+%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

By the divisor bound and the choice of ![Image 807: {D}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BD%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) we have

![Image 808: \displaystyle  \sum_{d \leq D} \tau_3(d) \tau_k(d) \ll_{k,C,\varepsilon} \frac{x}{\log^{k+1} x} ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bd+%5Cleq+D%7D+%5Ctau_3%28d%29+%5Ctau_k%28d%29+%5Cll_%7Bk%2CC%2C%5Cvarepsilon%7D+%5Cfrac%7Bx%7D%7B%5Clog%5E%7Bk%2B1%7D+x%7D+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

so it will suffice to show (after adjusting ![Image 809: {\varepsilon}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)) that

![Image 810: \displaystyle  \sum_{d \leq R} h(d) = \frac{1}{(2 + O(\varepsilon))^k k! {\mathfrak S}} \log^k x + O_{k,C,\varepsilon}( \log^{k-1} x ).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bd+%5Cleq+R%7D+h%28d%29+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B%282+%2B+O%28%5Cvarepsilon%29%29%5Ek+k%21+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D%7D+%5Clog%5Ek+x+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2C%5Cvarepsilon%7D%28+%5Clog%5E%7Bk-1%7D+x+%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

But from Theorem 27(ii) of [Notes 1](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2014/11/23/254a-notes-1-elementary-multiplicative-number-theory/) (with ![Image 811: {g(n)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bg%28n%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) replaced by ![Image 812: {n h(n)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn+h%28n%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)), the left-hand side is

![Image 813: \displaystyle  \frac{1}{k!} {\mathfrak H} \log^k R + O_{k,C,\varepsilon}( \log^{k-1} R )](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bk%21%7D+%7B%5Cmathfrak+H%7D+%5Clog%5Ek+R+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2C%5Cvarepsilon%7D%28+%5Clog%5E%7Bk-1%7D+R+%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

where

![Image 814: \displaystyle  {\mathfrak H} = \prod_p (1 - \frac{1}{p})^k (1 + h(p)).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7B%5Cmathfrak+H%7D+%3D+%5Cprod_p+%281+-+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%7D%29%5Ek+%281+%2B+h%28p%29%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Since ![Image 815: {h(p) = \frac{g(p)}{1-g(p)} = \frac{\omega(p)}{p-\omega(p)}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bh%28p%29+%3D+%5Cfrac%7Bg%28p%29%7D%7B1-g%28p%29%7D+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp-%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we see that ![Image 816: {{\mathfrak H} = {\mathfrak S}^{-1}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7B%5Cmathfrak+H%7D+%3D+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D%5E%7B-1%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and the claim follows (after adjusting ![Image 817: {\varepsilon}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) appropriately). ![Image 818: \Box](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5CBox&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

The upper bound in [(37)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#stab) is off by a factor of about ![Image 819: {2^k k!}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B2%5Ek+k%21%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) from what one expects to be the truth. The following exercises give some quick applications of Theorem [32](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#sieve-int).

> **Exercise 33 (Brun-Titchmarsh inequality)**[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) If ![Image 820: {\varepsilon > 0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon+%3E+0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and ![Image 821: {y}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is sufficiently large depending on ![Image 822: {\varepsilon}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), establish the [Brun-Titchmarsh inequality](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brun%E2%80%93Titchmarsh_theorem)
> 
> 
> ![Image 823: \displaystyle  \pi(x+y) - \pi(x) \leq \frac{(2+\varepsilon)y}{\log y}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cpi%28x%2By%29+-+%5Cpi%28x%29+%5Cleq+%5Cfrac%7B%282%2B%5Cvarepsilon%29y%7D%7B%5Clog+y%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)
> 
> 
> for all ![Image 824: {x > 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx+%3E+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), where ![Image 825: {\pi(x)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cpi%28x%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the number of primes less than ![Image 826: {x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). More generally, if ![Image 827: {\varepsilon > 0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon+%3E+0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 828: {a\ (q)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Ba%5C+%28q%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is a primitive residue class, and ![Image 829: {y}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is sufficiently large depending on ![Image 830: {\varepsilon,q}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon%2Cq%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), establish the Brun-Titchmarsh inequality
> 
> 
> ![Image 831: \displaystyle  \pi(x+y; a\ (q)) - \pi(x; a\ (q)) \leq \frac{(2+\varepsilon)y}{\phi(q) \log(y/q)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cpi%28x%2By%3B+a%5C+%28q%29%29+-+%5Cpi%28x%3B+a%5C+%28q%29%29+%5Cleq+%5Cfrac%7B%282%2B%5Cvarepsilon%29y%7D%7B%5Cphi%28q%29+%5Clog%28y%2Fq%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)
> 
> 
> for all ![Image 832: {x>1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx%3E1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), where ![Image 833: {\pi(x; a\ (q))}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cpi%28x%3B+a%5C+%28q%29%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the number of primes less than ![Image 834: {x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) that lie in ![Image 835: {a\ (q)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Ba%5C+%28q%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Comparing this with the prime number theorem in arithmetic progressions, we see that the Brun-Titchmarsh inequality is off from the truth by a factor of two when ![Image 836: {x = O(y)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx+%3D+O%28y%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002); however, the Brun-Titchmarsh inequality is applicable even when ![Image 837: {x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is extremely large compared with ![Image 838: {y}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), whereas the prime number theorem gives no non-trivial bound in this regime. The ![Image 839: {\varepsilon}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) can in fact be deleted in these inequalities, a [result of Montgomery and Vaughan](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=374060) (using a refinement of the large sieve, discussed below).

> **Exercise 34 (Upper bound for ![Image 840: {k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)-tuple conjecture)** If ![Image 841: {(h_1,\dots,h_k)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28h_1%2C%5Cdots%2Ch_k%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is an admissible ![Image 842: {k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)-tuple (as in Exercise [24](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#hak)), and let ![Image 843: {{\mathfrak S}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be the singular series [(27)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#lan). Show that [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> 
> [![Image 844: \displaystyle |\{ n \leq x: n+h_1,\dots,n+h_k \in {\mathcal P}\}| \leq (2^k k! + o(1)) {\mathfrak S} \frac{x}{\log^k x} \ \ \ \ \ (39)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7C%5C%7B+n+%5Cleq+x%3A+n%2Bh_1%2C%5Cdots%2Cn%2Bh_k+%5Cin+%7B%5Cmathcal+P%7D%5C%7D%7C+%5Cleq+%282%5Ek+k%21+%2B+o%281%29%29+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Cfrac%7Bx%7D%7B%5Clog%5Ek+x%7D+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2839%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) as ![Image 845: {x \rightarrow \infty}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx+%5Crightarrow+%5Cinfty%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). (This should be compared with the prime tuples conjecture, which predicts
> 
> 
> ![Image 846: \displaystyle  |\{ n \leq x: n+h_1,\dots,n+h_k \in {\mathcal P}\}| = (1 + o(1)) {\mathfrak S} \frac{x}{\log^k x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7C%5C%7B+n+%5Cleq+x%3A+n%2Bh_1%2C%5Cdots%2Cn%2Bh_k+%5Cin+%7B%5Cmathcal+P%7D%5C%7D%7C+%3D+%281+%2B+o%281%29%29+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Cfrac%7Bx%7D%7B%5Clog%5Ek+x%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)
> 
> 
> as ![Image 847: {x \rightarrow \infty}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx+%5Crightarrow+%5Cinfty%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002).)

As with the fundamental lemma, one can use the Selberg sieve together with the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem to sift out a set of primes, rather than a set of intervals:

> **Exercise 35 (Sieving primes)**[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) Let ![Image 848: {k, C}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%2C+C%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be fixed natural numbers, and let ![Image 849: {x \geq 2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx+%5Cgeq+2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). For each prime number ![Image 850: {p \leq \sqrt{x}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7Bx%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), let ![Image 851: {E_p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BE_p%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be the union of ![Image 852: {\omega(p)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) primitive residue classes modulo ![Image 853: {p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), where ![Image 854: {\omega(p) = k-1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Comega%28p%29+%3D+k-1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for all ![Image 855: {p \geq C}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp+%5Cgeq+C%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and ![Image 856: {\omega(p) < p-1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Comega%28p%29+%3C+p-1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for all ![Image 857: {p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Then for any ![Image 858: {\varepsilon > 0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon+%3E+0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), one has [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> 
> [![Image 859: \displaystyle |\{ p \in {\mathcal P}: p \leq x \} \backslash \bigcup_{p \leq \sqrt{x}} E_p| \leq (4^{k-1} (k-1)! + \varepsilon \ \ \ \ \ (40)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7C%5C%7B+p+%5Cin+%7B%5Cmathcal+P%7D%3A+p+%5Cleq+x+%5C%7D+%5Cbackslash+%5Cbigcup_%7Bp+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7Bx%7D%7D+E_p%7C+%5Cleq+%284%5E%7Bk-1%7D+%28k-1%29%21+%2B+%5Cvarepsilon+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2840%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
> ![Image 860: \displaystyle  + O_{k,C,\varepsilon}( \frac{1}{\log x} )) {\mathfrak S} \frac{x}{\log^k x} ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2C%5Cvarepsilon%7D%28+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B%5Clog+x%7D+%29%29+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Cfrac%7Bx%7D%7B%5Clog%5Ek+x%7D+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)
> 
> 
> where ![Image 861: {{\mathfrak S}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the singular series
> 
> 
> ![Image 862: \displaystyle  {\mathfrak S} := \prod_p (1-\frac{1}{p})^{1-k} (1 - \frac{\omega(p)}{p-1}).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%3A%3D+%5Cprod_p+%281-%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%7D%29%5E%7B1-k%7D+%281+-+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp-1%7D%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)
> 
> 
> (You will need Exercise 23 of [Notes 3](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/10/254a-notes-3-the-large-sieve-and-the-bombieri-vinogradov-theorem/) to control the ![Image 863: {\tau_3}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Ctau_3%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) terms arising from the remainder term.) If one assumes the [Elliott-Halberstam conjecture](http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliott%E2%80%93Halberstam_conjecture) (see Exercise 22 of [Notes 3](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/10/254a-notes-3-the-large-sieve-and-the-bombieri-vinogradov-theorem/)), show that the factor of ![Image 864: {4^{k-1}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B4%5E%7Bk-1%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) here can be lowered to ![Image 865: {2^{k-1}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B2%5E%7Bk-1%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002).

> **Exercise 36** Show that the quantity ![Image 866: {2^k k!}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B2%5Ek+k%21%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) in [(39)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#non) may be replaced by ![Image 867: {4^{k-1} (k-1)!}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B4%5E%7Bk-1%7D+%28k-1%29%21%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), which is a superior bound for ![Image 868: {k \leq 3}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk+%5Cleq+3%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). (It remains an open question to improve upon the bound ![Image 869: {\min( 2^k k!, 4^{k-1} (k-1)! )}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cmin%28+2%5Ek+k%21%2C+4%5E%7Bk-1%7D+%28k-1%29%21+%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) without assuming any further unproven conjectures.) Assuming the Elliott-Halberstam conjecture, show that one can replace ![Image 870: {2^k k!}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B2%5Ek+k%21%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) instead by ![Image 871: {2^{k-1} (k-1)!}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B2%5E%7Bk-1%7D+%28k-1%29%21%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002).

We have approached the analysis of the Selberg sieve as a “discrete” optimisation problem, in which one is trying to optimise the parameters ![Image 872: {(\rho_d)_{d|P; d \leq R}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28%5Crho_d%29_%7Bd%7CP%3B+d+%5Cleq+R%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) or ![Image 873: {(y_m)_{m|P; m \leq R}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28y_m%29_%7Bm%7CP%3B+m+%5Cleq+R%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) that are indexed by the discrete set ![Image 874: {\{ d|P: d \leq R \}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5C%7B+d%7CP%3A+d+%5Cleq+R+%5C%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). It is however also instructive to consider a “continuous” version of the optimisation problem, in which the coefficients ![Image 875: {\rho_d}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Crho_d%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) or ![Image 876: {y_m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7By_m%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) are described by a continuous function of ![Image 877: {d}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bd%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) or ![Image 878: {m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002); this is a slightly more restrictive class of sieves, but turns out to lead to numerical bounds that come very close to the discrete optimum, and allow for the use of methods from calculus of variations to come into play when using the Selberg sieve in more sophisticated ways. (See [these lecture notes of Selberg](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=1295844) for further discussion of how the continuous problem serves as a good approximation of the discrete one.) We give a basic illustration of this by presenting an alternate proof of (a very slightly weaker form of) Theorem [32](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#sieve-int) in which we do not use the choice [(35)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#opti) for the sieve weights ![Image 879: {\rho_e}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Crho_e%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), but instead choose weights of the form [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 880: \displaystyle \rho_d := \mu(d) f( \frac{\log d}{\log R} ) \ \ \ \ \ (41)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Crho_d+%3A%3D+%5Cmu%28d%29+f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2841%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) for some smooth compactly supported function ![Image 881: {f: {\bf R} \rightarrow {\bf R}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%3A+%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Crightarrow+%7B%5Cbf+R%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) that vanishes on ![Image 882: {[1,+\infty)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5B1%2C%2B%5Cinfty%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and which equals ![Image 883: {1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) at the origin; compare this smoothly truncated version of the Möbius function with the more abrupt truncations of the Möbius function that arise in combinatorial sieving. Such cases of the Selberg sieve were studied by several authors, most notably [Goldston, Pintz, and Yildirim](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=2552109) (although they made ![Image 884: {F}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BF%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) a polynomial on ![Image 885: {[0,1]}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5B0%2C1%5D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), rather than a smooth function). As in the previous proof of Theorem [32](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#sieve-int), we take ![Image 886: {g(p) := \frac{\omega(p)}{p}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bg%28p%29+%3A%3D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 887: {D := x^{1-\varepsilon}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BD+%3A%3D+x%5E%7B1-%5Cvarepsilon%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 888: {R := D^{1/2}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BR+%3A%3D+D%5E%7B1%2F2%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 889: {P := \prod_{p \leq \sqrt{x}} p}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BP+%3A%3D+%5Cprod_%7Bp+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7Bx%7D%7D+p%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), ![Image 890: {X=x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BX%3Dx%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and ![Image 891: {a_n := 1_{1 \leq n \leq x}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Ba_n+%3A%3D+1_%7B1+%5Cleq+n+%5Cleq+x%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). The quantity [(30)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dor) (or [(14)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dplus)) is then equal to

![Image 892: \displaystyle  \sum_{d_1,d_2|P} f( \frac{\log d_1}{\log R} ) f( \frac{\log d_2}{\log R} ) \mu(d_1) \mu(d_2) g( [d_1,d_2] ). ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bd_1%2Cd_2%7CP%7D+f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+%5Cmu%28d_1%29+%5Cmu%28d_2%29+g%28+%5Bd_1%2Cd_2%5D+%29.+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

We can remove the constraints ![Image 893: {d_1, d_2|P}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bd_1%2C+d_2%7CP%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), since the summands vanish outside of this range. To estimate this sum we use an argument related to that used to prove Proposition 9 from [Notes 2](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2014/12/09/254a-notes-2-complex-analytic-multiplicative-number-theory/). We first use Fourier inversion, applied to the function ![Image 894: {u \mapsto e^u f(u)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bu+%5Cmapsto+e%5Eu+f%28u%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), to obtain a representation of the form [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 895: \displaystyle e^u f(u) = \int_{\bf R} F(t) e^{-itu}\ dt \ \ \ \ \ (42)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++e%5Eu+f%28u%29+%3D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+F%28t%29+e%5E%7B-itu%7D%5C+dt+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2842%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) for some smooth, rapidly decreasing function ![Image 896: {F: {\bf R} \rightarrow {\bf C}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BF%3A+%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Crightarrow+%7B%5Cbf+C%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), thus [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 897: \displaystyle |F(t)| \ll_{f,A} (1+|t|)^{-A} \ \ \ \ \ (43)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7CF%28t%29%7C+%5Cll_%7Bf%2CA%7D+%281%2B%7Ct%7C%29%5E%7B-A%7D+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2843%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) for any ![Image 898: {A>0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BA%3E0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). We then have

![Image 899: \displaystyle  f( \frac{\log d_1}{\log R} ) = \int_{\bf R} \frac{F(t_1)}{d_1^{\frac{1+it_1}{\log R}}} dt_1](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+%3D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cfrac%7BF%28t_1%29%7D%7Bd_1%5E%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+dt_1&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and

![Image 900: \displaystyle  f( \frac{\log d_2}{\log R} ) = \int_{\bf R} \frac{F(t_2)}{d_2^{\frac{1+it_2}{\log R}}} dt_2](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+%3D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cfrac%7BF%28t_2%29%7D%7Bd_2%5E%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+dt_2&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and so by Fubini’s theorem (using the divisor bound ![Image 901: {g(d) = O( d^{-1+o(1)} )}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bg%28d%29+%3D+O%28+d%5E%7B-1%2Bo%281%29%7D+%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)) we may write [(30)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dor) as

![Image 902: \displaystyle  \int_{\bf R} \int_{\bf R} F(t_1) F(t_2) \sum_{d_1,d_2} \frac{\mu(d_1) \mu(d_2) g([d_1,d_2])}{d_1^{\frac{1+it_1}{\log R}} d_2^{\frac{1+it_2}{\log R}}}\ dt_1 dt_2,](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+F%28t_1%29+F%28t_2%29+%5Csum_%7Bd_1%2Cd_2%7D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Cmu%28d_1%29+%5Cmu%28d_2%29+g%28%5Bd_1%2Cd_2%5D%29%7D%7Bd_1%5E%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D+d_2%5E%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D%5C+dt_1+dt_2%2C&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and then by Euler products and the definition of ![Image 903: {g}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bg%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) we may factorise this as [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 904: \displaystyle \int_{\bf R} \int_{\bf R} F(t_1) F(t_2) G( t_1, t_2)\ dt_1 dt_2 \ \ \ \ \ (44)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+F%28t_1%29+F%28t_2%29+G%28+t_1%2C+t_2%29%5C+dt_1+dt_2+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2844%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) where

![Image 905: \displaystyle  G(t_1,t_2) := \prod_{p} ( 1 - \frac{\omega(p)}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_1}{\log R}}} - \frac{\omega(p)}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_2}{\log R}}} + \frac{\omega(p)}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_1+1+it_2}{\log R}}} ).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++G%28t_1%2Ct_2%29+%3A%3D+%5Cprod_%7Bp%7D+%28+1+-+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+-+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+%2B+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

We have the crude upper bound

![Image 906: \displaystyle  |G(t_1,t_2)| \ll_{k,C} \prod_p \exp( O_k( \frac{1}{p^{1+1/\log R}} ) )](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7CG%28t_1%2Ct_2%29%7C+%5Cll_%7Bk%2CC%7D+%5Cprod_p+%5Cexp%28+O_k%28+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B1%2F%5Clog+R%7D%7D+%29+%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

which by the asymptotic ![Image 907: {\sum_p \frac{1}{p^s} = -\log(s-1) + O(1)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Csum_p+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5Es%7D+%3D+-%5Clog%28s-1%29+%2B+O%281%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for ![Image 908: {s > 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bs+%3E+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) (see equation (22) of [Notes 1](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2014/11/23/254a-notes-1-elementary-multiplicative-number-theory/)) implies that

![Image 909: \displaystyle  |G(t_1,t_2)| \ll_{k,C} \log^{O_k(1)} R.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7CG%28t_1%2Ct_2%29%7C+%5Cll_%7Bk%2CC%7D+%5Clog%5E%7BO_k%281%29%7D+R.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

From this and the rapid decrease of ![Image 910: {f}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we see that the contribution of the cases ![Image 911: {|t_1| \geq \sqrt{\log R}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7Ct_1%7C+%5Cgeq+%5Csqrt%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) or ![Image 912: {|t_2| \geq \sqrt{\log R}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7Ct_2%7C+%5Cgeq+%5Csqrt%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) (say) to [(44)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#ffg) is ![Image 913: {O_{k,C,f,A}( \log^{-A} R )}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BO_%7Bk%2CC%2Cf%2CA%7D%28+%5Clog%5E%7B-A%7D+R+%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for any ![Image 914: {A>0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BA%3E0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Thus we can write [(44)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#ffg) as

![Image 915: \displaystyle  \int_{|t_1|, |t_2| \leq \sqrt{\log R}} F(t_1) F(t_2) G( t_1, t_2)\ dt_1 dt_2 + O_{k,C,f,A}(\log^{-A} R).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cint_%7B%7Ct_1%7C%2C+%7Ct_2%7C+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D+F%28t_1%29+F%28t_2%29+G%28+t_1%2C+t_2%29%5C+dt_1+dt_2+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2Cf%2CA%7D%28%5Clog%5E%7B-A%7D+R%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Since ![Image 916: {\zeta(s) = \prod_p (1 - \frac{1}{p^s})^{-1}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Czeta%28s%29+%3D+%5Cprod_p+%281+-+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5Es%7D%29%5E%7B-1%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we can factor

![Image 917: \displaystyle  G(t_1,t_2) = \frac{\zeta(1 + \frac{1+it_1+1+it_2}{\log R})^k}{\zeta(1+\frac{1+it_1}{\log R})^k \zeta(1+\frac{1+it_2}{\log R})^k} \prod_p E_p(t_1,t_2)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++G%28t_1%2Ct_2%29+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B%5Czeta%281+%2B+%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%29%5Ek%7D%7B%5Czeta%281%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%29%5Ek+%5Czeta%281%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%29%5Ek%7D+%5Cprod_p+E_p%28t_1%2Ct_2%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

where ![Image 918: {E_p(t_1,t_2)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BE_p%28t_1%2Ct_2%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the local Euler factor

![Image 919: \displaystyle  \frac{( 1 - \frac{\omega(p)}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_1}{\log R}}} - \frac{\omega(p)}{1+p^{\frac{1+it_2}{\log R}}} + \frac{\omega(p)}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_1+1+it_2}{\log R}}} ) (1 - \frac{1}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_1+1+it_2}{\log R}}})^k}{(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_1}{\log R}}})^k (1-\frac{1}{p^{1+\frac{1+it_2}{\log R}}})^k}.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cfrac%7B%28+1+-+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+-+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7B1%2Bp%5E%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+%2B+%5Cfrac%7B%5Comega%28p%29%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D+%29+%281+-+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D%29%5Ek%7D%7B%281-%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D%29%5Ek+%281-%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5E%7B1%2B%5Cfrac%7B1%2Bit_2%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D%7D%29%5Ek%7D.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

The point of this factorisation is that, thanks to a routine but somewhat tedious Taylor expansion, one has the asymptotics

![Image 920: \displaystyle  E_p(t_1,t_2) = 1 + O_{k,C}(\frac{1}{p^{3/2}})](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++E_p%28t_1%2Ct_2%29+%3D+1+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%7D%28%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5E%7B3%2F2%7D%7D%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

for any complex ![Image 921: {t_1,t_2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bt_1%2Ct_2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) with imaginary part less than ![Image 922: {\frac{1}{2} \log R}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B2%7D+%5Clog+R%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) in magnitude, and hence (by the Cauchy integral formula)

![Image 923: \displaystyle  \partial_{t_1} E_p(t_1,t_2), \partial_{t_2} E_p(t_1,t_2) = O_{k,C}( \frac{1}{\log R} \frac{1}{p^{3/2}})](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cpartial_%7Bt_1%7D+E_p%28t_1%2Ct_2%29%2C+%5Cpartial_%7Bt_2%7D+E_p%28t_1%2Ct_2%29+%3D+O_%7Bk%2CC%7D%28+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5E%7B3%2F2%7D%7D%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

for any complex ![Image 924: {t_1,t_2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bt_1%2Ct_2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) with imaginary part less than ![Image 925: {\frac{1}{4} \log R}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B4%7D+%5Clog+R%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) in magnitude. By the fundamental theorem of calculus, we thus have

![Image 926: \displaystyle  E_p(t_1,t_2) = E_p(i,i) + O_{k,C}( \frac{1}{\sqrt{\log R}} \frac{1}{p^{3/2}} )](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++E_p%28t_1%2Ct_2%29+%3D+E_p%28i%2Ci%29+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%7D%28+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B%5Csqrt%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bp%5E%7B3%2F2%7D%7D+%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

when ![Image 927: {|t_1|, |t_2| \leq \sqrt{\log R}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%7Ct_1%7C%2C+%7Ct_2%7C+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) are real. Also, observe from [(38)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#geop) that

![Image 928: \displaystyle  \prod_p E_p(i,i) = {\mathfrak S}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cprod_p+E_p%28i%2Ci%29+%3D+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and hence

![Image 929: \displaystyle  \prod_p E_p(t_1,t_2) = {\mathfrak S} + O_{k,C}( \frac{1}{\sqrt{\log R}} ).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cprod_p+E_p%28t_1%2Ct_2%29+%3D+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%7D%28+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B%5Csqrt%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D+%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

From this and the standard asymptotic ![Image 930: {\zeta(s) = \frac{1}{s-1} + O(1)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Czeta%28s%29+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bs-1%7D+%2B+O%281%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for ![Image 931: {s}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bs%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) close to ![Image 932: {1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we have

![Image 933: \displaystyle  G(t_1,t_2) = {\mathfrak S} \log^{-k} R \frac{(1+it_1)^k (1+it_2)^k}{(1+it_1+1+it_2)^k} + O_{k,C}( (1+|t_1|+|t_2|)^{O(1)} \log^{-k-1/2} R ) ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++G%28t_1%2Ct_2%29+%3D+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Clog%5E%7B-k%7D+R+%5Cfrac%7B%281%2Bit_1%29%5Ek+%281%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D%7B%281%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%7D%28+%281%2B%7Ct_1%7C%2B%7Ct_2%7C%29%5E%7BO%281%29%7D+%5Clog%5E%7B-k-1%2F2%7D+R+%29+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and thus we can write [(44)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#ffg) as

![Image 934: \displaystyle  {\mathfrak S} \log^{-k} R \int_{|t_1|, |t_2| \leq \sqrt{\log R}} F(t_1) F(t_2) \frac{(1+it_1)^k (1+it_2)^k}{(1+it_1+1+it_2)^k} \ dt_1 dt_2 ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Clog%5E%7B-k%7D+R+%5Cint_%7B%7Ct_1%7C%2C+%7Ct_2%7C+%5Cleq+%5Csqrt%7B%5Clog+R%7D%7D+F%28t_1%29+F%28t_2%29+%5Cfrac%7B%281%2Bit_1%29%5Ek+%281%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D%7B%281%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D+%5C+dt_1+dt_2+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

![Image 935: \displaystyle  + O_{k,C,f}( \log^{-k-1/2} R ).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2Cf%7D%28+%5Clog%5E%7B-k-1%2F2%7D+R+%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

From the rapid decrease of ![Image 936: {F}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BF%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we may now remove the constraints on ![Image 937: {t_1,t_2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bt_1%2Ct_2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) and write this as

![Image 938: \displaystyle  {\mathfrak S} \log^{-k} R \int_{\bf R} \int_{\bf R} F(t_1) F(t_2) \frac{(1+it_1)^k (1+it_2)^k}{(1+it_1+1+it_2)^k} \ dt_1 dt_2 ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Clog%5E%7B-k%7D+R+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+F%28t_1%29+F%28t_2%29+%5Cfrac%7B%281%2Bit_1%29%5Ek+%281%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D%7B%281%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D+%5C+dt_1+dt_2+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

![Image 939: \displaystyle + O_{k,C,f}( \log^{-k-1/2} R ).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2Cf%7D%28+%5Clog%5E%7B-k-1%2F2%7D+R+%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

To handle the weight ![Image 940: {\frac{(1+it_1)^k (1+it_2)^k}{(1+it_1+1+it_2)^k}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cfrac%7B%281%2Bit_1%29%5Ek+%281%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D%7B%281%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we observe from dividing [(42)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#euf) by ![Image 941: {e^u}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Be%5Eu%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) and then differentiating ![Image 942: {k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) times that

![Image 943: \displaystyle  f^{(k)}(u) = (-1)^k \int_{\bf R} (1+it)^k F(t) e^{-(1+it)u}\ dt](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++f%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29+%3D+%28-1%29%5Ek+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%281%2Bit%29%5Ek+F%28t%29+e%5E%7B-%281%2Bit%29u%7D%5C+dt&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

and hence by Fubini’s theorem

![Image 944: \displaystyle  f^{(k)}(u)^2 = \int_{\bf R} \int_{\bf R} (1+it_1)^k (1+it_2)^k F(t_1) F(t_2) e^{-(1+it_1+1+it_2)u}\ dt_1 dt_2.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++f%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29%5E2+%3D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%281%2Bit_1%29%5Ek+%281%2Bit_2%29%5Ek+F%28t_1%29+F%28t_2%29+e%5E%7B-%281%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%29u%7D%5C+dt_1+dt_2.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Using the Gamma function identity ![Image 945: {\int_0^\infty e^{-su} u^{k-1}\ du = \frac{(k-1)!}{s^k}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cint_0%5E%5Cinfty+e%5E%7B-su%7D+u%5E%7Bk-1%7D%5C+du+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B%28k-1%29%21%7D%7Bs%5Ek%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for any ![Image 946: {s}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bs%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) with ![Image 947: {\hbox{Re}(s)>0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Chbox%7BRe%7D%28s%29%3E0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we conclude from another application of Fubini’s theorem that

![Image 948: \displaystyle  \int_0^\infty f^{(k)}(u)^2 u^{k-1}\ du ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cint_0%5E%5Cinfty+f%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29%5E2+u%5E%7Bk-1%7D%5C+du+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

![Image 949: \displaystyle = (k-1)! \int_{\bf R} \int_{\bf R} \frac{(1+it_1)^k (1+it_2)^k}{(1+it_1+1+it_2)^k} F(t_1) F(t_2)\ dt_1 dt_2.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle+%3D+%28k-1%29%21+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cint_%7B%5Cbf+R%7D+%5Cfrac%7B%281%2Bit_1%29%5Ek+%281%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D%7B%281%2Bit_1%2B1%2Bit_2%29%5Ek%7D+F%28t_1%29+F%28t_2%29%5C+dt_1+dt_2.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Note from the support of ![Image 950: {f}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) that we can restrict the ![Image 951: {u}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bu%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) integral to ![Image 952: {1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Putting this all together, we see that with the choice of weights [(41)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#rhomf), the expression [(14)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dplus) takes the form

![Image 953: \displaystyle  \frac{1}{(k-1)!} {\mathfrak S} \log^{-k} R \int_0^1 f^{(k)}(u)^2 u^{k-1}\ du + O_{k,C,f}( \log^{-k-1/2} R ). ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7B%28k-1%29%21%7D+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Clog%5E%7B-k%7D+R+%5Cint_0%5E1+f%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29%5E2+u%5E%7Bk-1%7D%5C+du+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2Cf%7D%28+%5Clog%5E%7B-k-1%2F2%7D+R+%29.+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Recall that we are requiring ![Image 954: {f(0)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%280%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) to equal ![Image 955: {1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), which after ![Image 956: {k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) applications of the fundamental theorem of calculus (or integration by parts) is equivalent to the constraint

![Image 957: \displaystyle  \int_0^1 f^{(k)}(u) \frac{u^{k-1}}{(k-1)!}\ du = 1.](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cint_0%5E1+f%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29+%5Cfrac%7Bu%5E%7Bk-1%7D%7D%7B%28k-1%29%21%7D%5C+du+%3D+1.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Since ![Image 958: {\int_0^1 u^{k-1}\ du = \frac{1}{k}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cint_0%5E1+u%5E%7Bk-1%7D%5C+du+%3D+%5Cfrac%7B1%7D%7Bk%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we conclude from Cauchy-Schwarz that

![Image 959: \displaystyle  \int_0^1 f^{(k)}(u)^2 u^{k-1}\ du \geq k ((k-1)!)^2,](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cint_0%5E1+f%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29%5E2+u%5E%7Bk-1%7D%5C+du+%5Cgeq+k+%28%28k-1%29%21%29%5E2%2C&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

with equality occurring when ![Image 960: {f^{(k)}(u) = k!}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29+%3D+k%21%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Equality is not quite attainable with ![Image 961: {f}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) smooth, but by a mollification one can select an ![Image 962: {f}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) smoothly supported on (say) ![Image 963: {[-1,1]}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5B-1%2C1%5D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) with ![Image 964: {f(0)=0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%280%29%3D0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) and

![Image 965: \displaystyle  \int_0^1 f^{(k)}(u)^2 u^{k-1}\ du \leq (1+\varepsilon) k ((k-1)!)^2](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cint_0%5E1+f%5E%7B%28k%29%7D%28u%29%5E2+u%5E%7Bk-1%7D%5C+du+%5Cleq+%281%2B%5Cvarepsilon%29+k+%28%28k-1%29%21%29%5E2&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

for any ![Image 966: {\varepsilon > 0}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cvarepsilon+%3E+0%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). The expression [(14)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dplus) now takes the form

![Image 967: \displaystyle  (1+\varepsilon) k! {\mathfrak S} \log^{-k} R + O_{k,C,f}( \log^{-k-1/2} R ).](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%281%2B%5Cvarepsilon%29+k%21+%7B%5Cmathfrak+S%7D+%5Clog%5E%7B-k%7D+R+%2B+O_%7Bk%2CC%2Cf%7D%28+%5Clog%5E%7B-k-1%2F2%7D+R+%29.&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

Meanwhile, from [(41)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#rhomf) we have ![Image 968: {\rho_d = O_f(1)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Crho_d+%3D+O_f%281%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) and thus ![Image 969: {\lambda^+_d \ll_f \tau_3(d)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Clambda%5E%2B_d+%5Cll_f+%5Ctau_3%28d%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) much as before. We can then apply Theorem [5](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#dual-sieve) as before to recover Theorem [32](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#sieve-int).

> **Remark 37** The Selberg sieve is most naturally used as an upper bound sieve. However, it is possible to modify the Selberg sieve to produce a lower bound sieve in a number of ways. One way is to simply insert the Selberg sieve into the Buchstab identity [(16)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#geo); see for instance [this paper of Ankeny and Onishi](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=167467) for an investigation of this approach. Another method is to multiply the Selberg upper bound sieve by a lower bound sieve ![Image 970: {\Lambda_-}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5CLambda_-%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002); somewhat surprisingly, even a very crude lower bound sieve such as the ![Image 971: {k=1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%3D1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) Bonferroni sieve ![Image 972: {\Lambda_- = 1 - \sum_{p|P} 1_{E_p}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5CLambda_-+%3D+1+-+%5Csum_%7Bp%7CP%7D+1_%7BE_p%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) can give reasonably good results for this purpose. See [Friedlander and Iwaniec](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=2647984) for further analysis of this lower bound sieve (sometimes known as the ![Image 973: {\Lambda^2 \Lambda_-}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5CLambda%5E2+%5CLambda_-%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) sieve).

**— 3. A multidimensional Selberg sieve, and bounded gaps between primes —**

We now discuss a multidimensional variant of the Selberg sieve that establishes a [recent result of Maynard](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=3272929) (and independently by myself), giving further partial progress towards the Hardy-Littlewood prime tuples conjecture. For sake of exposition we shall omit some details; see [this previous blog post](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2013/11/19/polymath8b-bounded-intervals-with-many-primes-after-maynard/) for a more careful treatment; see also [this survey of Granville](http://arxiv.org/abs/1410.8400).

More precisely, we sketch the proof of

> **Theorem 38 (Maynard’s theorem)**[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) Let ![Image 974: {m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be a natural number, and let ![Image 975: {k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be sufficiently large depending on ![Image 976: {m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Then for any admissible ![Image 977: {k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bk%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)-tuple ![Image 978: {(h_1,\dots,h_k)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%28h_1%2C%5Cdots%2Ch_k%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), there exist infinitely many natural numbers ![Image 979: {n}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) such that at least ![Image 980: {m+1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%2B1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) of the numbers ![Image 981: {n+h_1,\dots,n+h_k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn%2Bh_1%2C%5Cdots%2Cn%2Bh_k%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) are prime.

> **Exercise 39** Show that Maynard’s theorem implies that the quantity ![Image 982: {H_{m} = \lim \inf_{n \rightarrow \infty} p_{n+m}-p_n}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BH_%7Bm%7D+%3D+%5Clim+%5Cinf_%7Bn+%5Crightarrow+%5Cinfty%7D+p_%7Bn%2Bm%7D-p_n%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is finite for each natural number ![Image 983: {m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), where ![Image 984: {p_n}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp_n%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is the ![Image 985: {n^{th}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn%5E%7Bth%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) prime, is finite. (For the most recent bounds on ![Image 986: {H_m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BH_m%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), see [this Polymath paper](http://www.resmathsci.com/content/1/1/12).) In particular, the ![Image 987: {m=1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%3D1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) case of Theorem [38](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#may) yields “bounded gaps between primes”, in that there is a finite ![Image 988: {H}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BH%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for which ![Image 989: {p_{n+1}-p_n \leq H}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bp_%7Bn%2B1%7D-p_n+%5Cleq+H%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for infinitely many ![Image 990: {n}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002).

Even the ![Image 991: {m=1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%3D1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) case of this theorem was only proven in 2013 [by Zhang](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=3171761), building upon [earlier work of Goldston, Pintz, and Yildirim](http://www.ams.org/mathscinet-getitem?mr=2552109). Zhang’s original value of ![Image 992: {H}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BH%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) in the “bounded gaps” application was ![Image 993: {H = 7 \times 10^7}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BH+%3D+7+%5Ctimes+10%5E7%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002); this has since been lowered several times, with the current record being ![Image 994: {H=246}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BH%3D246%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), [due to the Polymath project](http://www.resmathsci.com/content/1/1/12). The original arguments of Zhang and Goldston-Pintz-Yildirim used a “one-dimensional” Selberg sieve, with coefficients given by [(41)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#rhomf), and required quite strong equidistribution hypotheses on the primes (stronger than what the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem provides). However, the argument of Maynard relies on a more general and flexible “multi-dimensional” Selberg sieve, which has better numerical performance, and as such does not need any equidistribution result beyond the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem. This sieve has since been used for a number of further applications in analytic number theory; see [this web page](http://michaelnielsen.org/polymath1/index.php?title=Bounded_gaps_between_primes) for a list of papers in this direction.

Let ![Image 995: {m, k, h_1,\dots,h_k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%2C+k%2C+h_1%2C%5Cdots%2Ch_k%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) be as in Theorem [38](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#may). Suppose that, for sufficiently large ![Image 996: {x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), we can find a non-negative function ![Image 997: {\nu: {\bf N} \rightarrow {\bf R}^+}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cnu%3A+%7B%5Cbf+N%7D+%5Crightarrow+%7B%5Cbf+R%7D%5E%2B%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) supported on ![Image 998: {[x/2,x]}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Bx%2F2%2Cx%5D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) with the property that [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 999: \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^k \sum_n \nu(n) 1_{n+h_i \in {\mathcal P}} > m \sum_n \nu(n). \ \ \ \ \ (45)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Csum_%7Bi%3D1%7D%5Ek+%5Csum_n+%5Cnu%28n%29+1_%7Bn%2Bh_i+%5Cin+%7B%5Cmathcal+P%7D%7D+%3E+m+%5Csum_n+%5Cnu%28n%29.+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2845%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
[](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/) Then by the pigeonhole principle, there exists at least one ![Image 1000: {n \in [x/2,x]}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn+%5Cin+%5Bx%2F2%2Cx%5D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) such that ![Image 1001: {\sum_{i=1}^k 1_{n+h_i \in {\mathcal P}} > m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Csum_%7Bi%3D1%7D%5Ek+1_%7Bn%2Bh_i+%5Cin+%7B%5Cmathcal+P%7D%7D+%3E+m%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), that is to say at least ![Image 1002: {m+1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%2B1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) of the numbers ![Image 1003: {n+h_1,\dots,n+h_k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn%2Bh_1%2C%5Cdots%2Cn%2Bh_k%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) are prime. Letting ![Image 1004: {x}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bx%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) go to infinity (keeping ![Image 1005: {m,k,h_1,\dots,h_k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%2Ck%2Ch_1%2C%5Cdots%2Ch_k%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) fixed), we will obtain Theorem [38](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#may).

It remains to establish the existence of a function ![Image 1006: {\nu}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cnu%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) with the required properties. Prior to the work of Goldston, Pintz, and Yildirim, it was only known (using tools such as the fundamental lemma of sieve theory, or off-the-shelf Selberg sieves) how to construct sieve weights ![Image 1007: {\nu}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cnu%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) obeying [(45)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#nund) with ![Image 1008: {m}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) replaced by a small constant less than ![Image 1009: {1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). The earlier arguments of Zhang and Goldston-Pintz-Yildirim chose a weight ![Image 1010: {\nu}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Cnu%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) which was essentially of the Selberg sieve form

![Image 1011: \displaystyle  \nu(n) := 1_{[x/2,x]}(n) \left( \sum_{d | (n+h_1) \dots (n+h_k)} \mu(d) f( \frac{\log d}{\log R} ) \right)^2](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cnu%28n%29+%3A%3D+1_%7B%5Bx%2F2%2Cx%5D%7D%28n%29+%5Cleft%28+%5Csum_%7Bd+%7C+%28n%2Bh_1%29+%5Cdots+%28n%2Bh_k%29%7D+%5Cmu%28d%29+f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+%5Cright%29%5E2&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

(compare with [(41)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#rhomf)), where ![Image 1012: {R = x^{\theta/2}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7BR+%3D+x%5E%7B%5Ctheta%2F2%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for some fixed ![Image 1013: {0 < \theta < 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B0+%3C+%5Ctheta+%3C+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and ![Image 1014: {f}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) was a smooth fixed compactly supported function. Omitting many calculations, their conclusion was that one could prove the ![Image 1015: {m=1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%3D1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) case of Theorem [38](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#may) once one had some sort of distribution result on the primes at level ![Image 1016: {\theta}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Ctheta%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) (as defined in Exercise 22 of [Notes 3](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/10/254a-notes-3-the-large-sieve-and-the-bombieri-vinogradov-theorem/)) with ![Image 1017: {1/2 < \theta < 1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B1%2F2+%3C+%5Ctheta+%3C+1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). Unfortunately, this just falls short of what the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem provides, which is a level of distribution ![Image 1018: {\theta}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Ctheta%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) for any ![Image 1019: {0 < \theta < 1/2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B0+%3C+%5Ctheta+%3C+1%2F2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002). One of the key contributions of Zhang was to obtain a partial distribution result at a value of ![Image 1020: {\theta}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5Ctheta%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) slightly above ![Image 1021: {1/2}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B1%2F2%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), which when combined with (a modification of) the previous work of Goldston, Pintz, and Yildirim, was able to yield the ![Image 1022: {m=1}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bm%3D1%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) case of Theorem [38](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/#may).

The approach of Maynard and myself was a little different, based on multidimensional Selberg sieves such as

![Image 1023: \displaystyle  \nu(n) := 1_{[x/2,x]}(n) \times](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cnu%28n%29+%3A%3D+1_%7B%5Bx%2F2%2Cx%5D%7D%28n%29+%5Ctimes&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

![Image 1024: \displaystyle  \left( \sum_{d_i | n+h_i \forall i=1,\dots,k} \mu(d_1) \dots \mu(d_k) f( \frac{\log d_1}{\log R}, \dots, \frac{\log d_k}{\log R} ) \right)^2](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cleft%28+%5Csum_%7Bd_i+%7C+n%2Bh_i+%5Cforall+i%3D1%2C%5Cdots%2Ck%7D+%5Cmu%28d_1%29+%5Cdots+%5Cmu%28d_k%29+f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%2C+%5Cdots%2C+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_k%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+%5Cright%29%5E2&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)

for some multidimensional smooth function ![Image 1025: {f: [0,+\infty)^k \rightarrow {\bf R}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%3A+%5B0%2C%2B%5Cinfty%29%5Ek+%5Crightarrow+%7B%5Cbf+R%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) supported on the simplex ![Image 1026: {\{ (t_1,\dots,t_k): t_1+\dots +t_k \leq 1 \}}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7B%5C%7B+%28t_1%2C%5Cdots%2Ct_k%29%3A+t_1%2B%5Cdots+%2Bt_k+%5Cleq+1+%5C%7D%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002); the one-dimensional sieve considered by Zhang and Goldston-Pintz-Yildirim is then essentially the case when ![Image 1027: {f(t_1,\dots,t_k)}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bf%28t_1%2C%5Cdots%2Ct_k%29%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) is a function just of ![Image 1028: {t_1+\dots+t_k}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bt_1%2B%5Cdots%2Bt_k%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002) on this simplex. Actually, to avoid some (very minor) issues concerning common factors between the ![Image 1029: {n+h_i}](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%7Bn%2Bh_i%7D&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002), and also to essentially eliminate the role of the singular series, it is convenient to work with a slight modification [](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)

[![Image 1030: \displaystyle \nu(n) := 1_{n=b\ (W)} 1_{[x/2,x]}(n) \times \ \ \ \ \ (46)](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cnu%28n%29+%3A%3D+1_%7Bn%3Db%5C+%28W%29%7D+1_%7B%5Bx%2F2%2Cx%5D%7D%28n%29+%5Ctimes+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%5C+%2846%29&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2015/01/21/254a-notes-4-some-sieve-theory/)
![Image 1031: \displaystyle  \left( \sum_{d_i | n+h_i \forall i=1,\dots,k} \mu(d_1) \dots \mu(d_k) f( \frac{\log d_1}{\log R}, \dots, \frac{\log d_k}{\log R} ) \right)^2 ](https://s0.wp.com/latex.php?latex=%5Cdisplaystyle++%5Cleft%28+%5Csum_%7Bd_i+%7C+n%2Bh_i+%5Cforall+i%3D1%2C%5Cdots%2Ck%7D+%5Cmu%28d_1%29+%5Cdots+%5Cmu%28d_k%29+f%28+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_1%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D%2C+%5Cdots%2C+%5Cfrac%7B%5Clog+d_k%7D%7B%5Clog+R%7D+%29+%5Cright%29%5E2+&bg=ffffff&fg=000000&s=0&c=20201002)
